F2 — BLOCKING: model_response is declared passing evidence without resolving the echoed-request caveat
Draft location: spec §1.1 item 1, lines 106–108, and §2.6 item 1, lines 228–235; plan §3 M0, lines 285–288, and §5 item 1, lines 366–375.
`drafts/2026-09-19-phase-3b-live-tui-integration-design.md`, lines 228–235:

```
1. **Identity primitive.** madv-surface's passing evidence under 3A §2.2 is its
   structured per-response metadata: the `model_response` event, emitted after
   every provider response and before any tool runs, carrying
   `response_model` copied from the server's own field, `response_id`,
   `tls_verified_host`, and `response_maker` and
   `response_independence_domain` resolved from `response_model` alone.
   `system/init` and requested-model fields are requested intent and are
   failing evidence.

```

`tui/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-phase-3a-runtime-foundation-design.md`, lines 281–286:

```
**Passing evidence**

- a documented CLI or API primitive returning the resolved provider and exact
  model for the actual session; or
- structured per-session or per-response metadata naming the provider and
  exact model actually used.

```

`tui/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-phase-3a-runtime-foundation-design.md`, lines 288–297:

```
**Failing evidence**

- configuration files;
- requested flags or environment values;
- marketing names or version banners;
- unstructured prose;
- behavioral inference;
- cached attestations; or
- any statement of requested intent rather than resolved fact.

```

`surface/docs/provider-fields.md`, lines 115–120:

```
Identity caveat: Moonshot documents the response `model` as echoing the
request, the Kimi Code page maps `kimi-for-coding` to the model version "K2.8
Preview", and the models page warns "A mistyped model ID falls back to K2.8
Preview without thinking" (read 2026-11-05). A reviewer may judge this field
to be configuration echoed back rather than resolved fact. Which ids the
coding host accepts is now settled by the Kimi Code page's list of six.

```

`surface/docs/provider-fields.md`, lines 377–389:

```
- `endpoint_host`, `profile`/`provider` and `billing_guard` in events are
  configuration (the pinned profile). `tls_verified_host`, `response_model`,
  `response_id`, `request_id` and `usage` are taken from the connection or the
  server. `tls_verified_host` is recorded per TLS socket at its handshake, so
  every HTTPS response carries it, including those on a reused connection.
- `response_maker` and `response_independence_domain` are a lookup of the
  server's model field in a configured family table: the model id is fact,
  the maker behind the family is documentation read on 2026-11-04 and
  2026-11-05. A provider that reuses a family name for another maker's model,
  or serves a model under a different id than the maker's, would be
  mis-resolved. The four multi-maker hosts (`qwen`, `ollama`, `opencode-go`,
  `mistral`) name the maker for some families only; the others are cited from
  the maker's own naming or press coverage above.

```

The event structure is a candidate passing primitive. Its presence alone does not prove that the field identifies the exact model actually used. In particular, the locally supplied Kimi caveat describes requested intent and fallback. TLS authenticates an endpoint; a response id correlates a response; a configured family lookup labels a model string. None converts an echoed request into resolved identity. This is not a demand to prove a provider cannot lie: Phase 3A explicitly accepts that residual. It is a demand to distinguish documented resolved fact from documented intent.
Fix: make M0's outcome conditional on a profile-specific investigation proving actual-session/exact-model semantics under §2.2. Record the primitive and its limits, reject echoes/aliases/unresolved evidence, and halt admission if no qualifying primitive exists. Keep Claude Code plus madv-surface as the chosen pair; change the admission evidence or eligible configuration within that pair.

```
export function parseAuthReadiness(output: string): boolean {
  try {
    const v: unknown = JSON.parse(output);
    if (v === null || typeof v !== "object" || Array.isArray(v)) return false;
    const p = v as Record<string, unknown>;
    return p.loggedIn === true && p.authMethod === "claude.ai"
      && p.apiProvider === "firstParty" && p.subscriptionType === "max";
  } catch { return false; }

`tui/packages/protocol/src/capability-record.ts`, lines 364–374:

```
  const nowMs = canonicalTimestampMs(now, "invalid_now", "now");
  if (record.binary_sha256 !== observed.binary_sha256) return stale("binary_hash_changed");
  if (record.cli_version !== observed.cli_version) return stale("cli_version_changed");
  for (const field of HOST_FIELDS) {
    if (record.host[field] !== observed.host[field]) return stale("host_changed");

  // strictly before" so a record built without the parser whose expires_at
  // does not parse reads as expired, never fresh.

```
  async function request(command: BrokerCommand): Promise<BrokerResult> {
    // Evaluate and dispatch the same values even when the caller supplies getters.
    const captured = { ...command };
    const commandId = captured.commandId;
    const snapshot = projectSnapshot(broker.snapshotProjectionInput());
    const legality = evaluateCommandLegality(captured, snapshot, evaluatedPrincipal);
    if (!legality.ok) {
      return { ok: false, commandId, error: legality.error, detail: legality.detail };
    }
    switch (captured.kind) {
      case "session_pause":
        await broker.pause(commandId);
        break;
      case "session_resume":
        await broker.resume(commandId);
        break;
      case "session_close":
        await broker.founderClose(commandId);
        break;
    }
    return { ok: true, commandId, acceptedSnapshotSeq: deliveredSeq };
  }

Three dependencies are unresolved. `tui/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-phase-3a-runtime-foundation-design.md`, lines 225–245:

```
#### Pure preflight

Pure preflight:

1. parses and validates the task envelope;
2. validates exactly two declared `ExecutionIdentity` records;
3. fingerprints the repository and worktree;
4. resolves each executable to an absolute path and hashes its artifact;
5. runs fresh non-mutating, non-interactive identity and auth-readiness probes;
6. validates exact provider and exact model against the envelope;
7. validates pair-level eligibility;
8. proposes and validates the storage root without creating it; and
9. validates host/platform capabilities required for the requested pair.

Preflight creates nothing in governed storage, the repository, ledger, or
session state. Provider CLI subprocesses may touch their own provider-owned
caches or state; Phase 3A neither relies on nor represents those effects as
governed writes.

Every probe runs under the exact same per-adapter allowlisted environment that
the PTY host and governed child will receive. Probe environment must equal

```

A healthy production composition must perform fresh identity and auth probes, which can call providers before any user task. The closed live-call list already forbids those calls in M1–M4. Those slices can proceed with offline controlled evidence; that evidence must not be presented as a live production run. This is a clarification, not permission to broaden the live-call list.
Fix: label M1–M4 transcripts as offline and define the controlled test procedure. Reserve actual production proof for the authorized live slices and the final candidate procedure of F7. If the Founder instead wants live children earlier, amend the list explicitly and name every identity/auth/reattest call, subscription, guard and terms prerequisite. Include startup and failure-path calls on both surfaces and hosts, not only user prompts.
F9 — BLOCKING: the spec overstates which identity failures exit 4

```
2. **Live attestation.** In a governed session the adapter launches
   madv-surface with the registered profile, the envelope's exact model and
   `--require-model`, so that any response naming another model, or resolving
   to another independence domain, ends the process with exit 4 before any

`surface/src/errors.ts`, lines 22–30:

```
const KIND_EXIT: Record<FailKind, number> = {
  identity_unavailable: EXIT.IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE,
  identity_mismatch: EXIT.IDENTITY_MISMATCH,
  auth_failed: EXIT.AUTH_FAILED,

Anthropic and unresolved-family responses are rejected before the require-model/domain-mismatch checks and exit 7. Missing model evidence exits 3. Only after preflight succeeds does the build phase:

`tui/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-phase-3a-runtime-foundation-design.md`, lines 741–750:

```
Forwarding is not custody. A secret-bearing provider variable may be forwarded
from the ambient host into an allowlisted child. MADVentures runtime code does
not intentionally log, persist, or export its value. Secret-marked values are
redacted from diagnostic output, errors, environment reports, and evidence.
Variable names may be recorded; values may not.

Identity probes, auth-readiness probes, PTY hosts, and governed children receive
the same exact allowlisted environment. An acceptance fixture dumps each
environment and asserts four-way equality and the absence of every ambient
variable not on the allowlist.

```

`tui/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-phase-3a-runtime-foundation-design.md`, lines 788–798:

```
### 5.6 Evidence and redaction limits

The system never intentionally collects or records authentication flows or
credentials of its own doing. Sanitized exports apply best-effort redaction for
known token patterns and exact allowlisted secret values and record which rules
ran.

PTY snapshots necessarily contain provider-controlled output. An agent or
provider may print a secret despite the supervisor's rules. Agent-printed
secrets are a documented residual; Phase 3A does not claim exhaustive
secret-free evidence.

```

Marking the key secret is the right forwarding rule. It does not by itself choose whether the CLI's default transcript is retained, nor demonstrate redaction of the newly forwarded value through diagnostics and evidence. The inherited best-effort/output residual should remain visible.
Fix: choose and pin the persistence flag, record any provider-owned transcript retention, and use synthetic secret canaries to exercise the new allowlist through the same probe/host/child path and sanitized evidence export. Do not collect real key values for environment-equality evidence or imply exhaustive secret removal.

== part 3 of 5 (36538 characters)
on will trace the end-to-end data flow of a Phase 4 planning round: 1. Room creation and `T1` (`scope.captured`): The caller creates a room (`POST /rooms`) and posts `T1` (`POST /rooms/:roomId/events`) with `scopePaths` and `goal`; the server computes `founderGoalDigest(goal)` and stores the `founder_goal` evidence item in `room_events`. 2. Round opening (`POST /control-plane/rooms/:roomId/planner-round` or `PlannerLoop.runRound`): If the room is non-terminal in the journal, `PlannerRecovery.recoverRoom` runs first (`journal.verify()` + `planner.reconcile(commandId)`). Otherwise, under the room lock (L3), if the room is in `SCOPED`, `PlannerLoop` appends `T2` (`task.dispatched.planner`, transitioning `SCOPED -> PLANNING` with G2 guard facts) and, in the same database transaction (`afterTransition` taking journal lock L4), appends the `journaled` row for deterministic `cmd_planner_<32-hex>`. If the room is already in `PLANNING` after `T4` (`plan.revision_requested`) or a prior `not_sent` attempt within the 3-attempt budget (`DISPATCH_POLICY.transport_retries_max = 2`), it journals the next attempt bound to the opening `T2` or `T4` event. 3. Route authorization and pre-execution attestation: `PlannerLoop` checks `authorizeRoute` against the active `AuthorizationDocument` and calls `GatewayPlannerChannel.attest(commandId)`. On the next `POST /gateway/planner/poll`, `GatewayPlannerWorker` verifies or creates the durable command record in `<gatewayDirectory>/planner/` and calls `AgyPlannerExecutor.attest`, which checks that no lingering process exists for `BUILDROOM_PLANNER_USER` and verifies the SHA-256 of `BUILDROOM_PLANNER_AGY`, returning seat `architect`, surface `antigravity`, provider `google`, and model `gemini-4-argon`. The control plane verifies exact identity match and journals `identity_bound`. 4. Dispatch and stream-json init gate: `PlannerLoop` calls `GatewayPlannerChannel.dispatch(work)` with captured `repository`, `baseSha`, `scopePaths`, `goal`, and optional latest `T4` `revisionFeedback`. On `execute`, `AgyPlannerExecutor` creates a read-only checkout at `baseSha`, spawns `sudo -n -u <plannerUser> -- <agyPath>` with `--mode plan --sandbox --model gemini-4-argon-high --effort high`, and waits for the stream-json `init` event before writing durable running evidence (`<gatewayDirectory>/planner-evidence/`) and writing the prompt to stdin. If anything fails before stdin is written, it returns `not_sent`; once `init` is verified (`init.model === 'gemini-4-argon-high'` and non-empty `conversation_id`), the Gateway reports `accepted` with `{ initModel, conversationId }` and the control plane journals `dispatched` (`BRJ:c:2`). 5. Plan completion, judgment, and atomic `T3`: When `agy` exits, `AgyPlannerExecutor` kills any remaining planner-user processes (`pkill -KILL`), removes the checkout, writes closing evidence, and returns the raw assistant text. `GatewayPlannerWorker` records `finished` and reports the parsed output on a subsequent poll/report cycle. `PlannerLoop` runs `judgeRoundPlan` (checking `PLANDOC_SCHEMA_V1`, canonical `scope_paths` within captured room scope, exact `repository` and `base_sha`, `BRJ:b:1` `planHash`, `PLAN_READY_FOR_FOUNDER_APPROVAL`, and credential redaction) and then executes `submitRoundPlan`, which commits `T3` (`plan.submitted`, `PLANNING -> PLAN_REVIEW`), `PlanStore.recordPlan` (`build_room_plans` with `xmin` check), and the journal `completed` row in a single Postgres transaction. 6. Founder inspection and decision: The Founder reads the plan via `GET /control-plane/rooms/:roomId/plan` (or the `plan-<planHash>.json` file written by the harness) and submits `POST /control-plane/rooms/:roomId/plan-decision` with `PHASE3_ADJUDICATION_TOKEN`, atomically recording `T5` (`plan.approved` -> `APPROVED`), `T4` (`plan.revision_requested` -> `PLANNING` for the next round), or `T22` (`founder.cancel` -> `CANCELLED`) together with the bound `BRJ:d:1` `DecisionRecordRow`.",

  "data_flow": "1. **Goal capture (`ROOM_CREATED -> SCOPED`)**:\n   - A room is created in `build_room_rooms` (`ROOM_CREATED`). `recordFounderGoal` (`POST /control-plane/rooms/:roomId/goal`) appends `T1` (`scope.captured`) to `build_room_events`, capturing `goal`, `repository`, `baseSha`, `scopePaths`, and `controllingInputs` and moving the room to `SCOPED`.\n\n2. **Pre-dispatch attestation and journaled dispatch (`SCOPED | PLANNING -> PLANNING`)**:\n   - `PlannerLoop.runRound(roomId)` reads the room state and round inputs (`readRoundInputs`). FROM build_room_rooms WHERE room_id = $1 FOR UPDATE`), `openRoundDispatch` appends `T2` (`task.dispatched.planner`) in Round 1 (moving `SCOPED -> PLANNING`) or reuses the Round 1 `T2` event id on revision rounds (`requireSameTransaction: false`), and appends `command.dispatched` to `command_journal_events` via `JournalStore.inTransaction(client).appendCommandEvent` (`command_journal_append` locks `command_journal_chain_head` at L2).\n\n3. **Gateway execution (`AgyPlannerExecutor`)**:\n   - Only after the `command.dispatched` transaction commits does `PlannerLoop` call `planner.dispatch(work)` and `planner.awaitPlan(commandId)`.\n   - When `AgyPlannerExecutor.start(work, identity)` runs (`startHeld`), it checks `rejectIfSent(work.commandId)` against `planner-evidence/<sha256(commandId)>.json`, runs `pgrep -u <plannerUser>` (`assertNoPlannerUserProcesses`) to ensure no lingering processes exist for the isolated planner OS user, hashes `agyPath` with `sha256OfFile` against `agySha256`, creates a read-only anonymous shallow git checkout at `work.baseSha` (`chmod` `0555` on directories and `0444` on files), and spawns `sudo -n -u <plannerUser> -- <agyPath>` with `--input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --model gemini-4-argon-high --effort high --mode plan --sandbox --print-timeout 900s`.\n   - `AgyPlannerExecutor` holds back the prompt until the first stdout stream-json line is an `init` event naming `model: 'gemini-4-argon-high'` and a non-empty `conversation_id`, writes durable running evidence (`sent: true`) to `<evidenceDir>/<sha256(commandId)>.json`, and only then writes `agyInputLine(plannerPrompt(work))` to stdin.\n   - Across all stream-json events, `AgyRun.judge` verifies no event names a model other than `gemini-4-argon-high`.   "error_and_failure_behavior": "The `README.md` section documents the exact failure classifications and refusal behaviors implemented in the codebase:\n1. **Pre-dispatch failures (before `command.dispatched` is journaled)**:\n   - Unresolved prior planner command in the room returns `{ outcome: 'blocked_unresolved_command', commandId }`.\n   - Attestation failure (`planner.attest` error or invalid `ExecutionIdentity`) returns `{ outcome: 'attestation_failed', reason }` with no `T2` event and no journal row written.\n2. **Post-dispatch failures (after `command.dispatched` is committed)**:\n   - Every failure appends `command.failed` to `command_journal_events` with one of the closed classifications in `PlannerFailureClassification`: `'dispatch_refused'`, `'transport_lost'`, `'execution_failed'`, `'plan_rejected'`, `'plan_not_ready'`, `'plan_out_of_scope'`, `'plan_refused'`, `'gateway_reconciled'`, or `'submission_failed'`, leaving the room in `PLANNING`.\n   - Specifically: `judgePlanDoc` schema or canonical path rejection -> `'plan_rejected'`; def kind(ch): return 'brace' if ch in '{}' else 'fence' if ch == '`' else 'bracket' if ch in '[]' else 'letter' if ch.isalpha() else 'digit' if ch.isdigit() else 'space' if ch.isspace() else 'other'

def repair_escapes(s):
    out, k, n = [], 0, 0
    while k < len(s):
        ch = s[k]
        if ch != '\\': out.append(ch); k += 1; continue
        nx = s[k+1:k+2]
        if nx and nx in '"\\/bfnrt': out.append(s[k:k+2]); k += 2; continue
        if nx == 'u' and re.match(r'[0-9a-fA-F]{4}', s[k+2:k+6]): out.append(s[k:k+6]); k += 6; continue
        out.append('\\\\'); k += 1; n += 1
    return ''.join(out), n
def repair_controls(s):
    out, ins, esc, n = [], False, False, 0
    for ch in s:
        if ins:
            if esc: esc = False; out.append(ch); continue
            if ch == '\\': esc = True; out.append(ch); continue
            if ch == '"': ins = False; out.append(ch); continue
            if ord(ch) < 0x20: out.append('\\u%04x' % ord(ch)); n += 1; continue
            out.append(ch)
        else:
            if ch == '"': ins = True
            out.append(ch)
    return ''.join(out), n
def tryparse(label, s):
    try:
        v = json.loads(s); print(label, 'parses:', type(v).__name__, ('keys %d' % len(v)) if isinstance(v, dict) else '')
    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
        print(label, 'does not parse:', e.msg, '| line', e.lineno, 'column', e.colno, 'char', e.pos, 'of', len(s))

re_t, n_esc = repair_escapes(body); print('backslashes the executor repair would change:', n_esc)
tryparse('B after the executor repair', re_t)
rc_t, n_ctl = repair_controls(body); print('raw control characters inside strings:', n_ctl)
tryparse('C with raw control characters escaped', rc_t)
both, _ = repair_controls(re_t); tryparse('D both repairs', both)
if fb >= 0 and lb > fb: tryparse('E text from first brace to last brace', t[fb:lb+1])

GUARD = re.compile(r'ya29\.|bearer\s|refresh_token|access_token|id_token|client_secret|api[_-]?key|oauth|authorization|passw|cookie|token', re.I)
KEEP = ('error', 'reason', 'code', 'status_code', 'statusCode', 'exit_code', 'exitCode')
def kept(o, out):
    if isinstance(o, dict):
        for k, v in o.items():
            if k in KEEP and isinstance(v, (str, int, float)):
                s = str(v)
                out.append(k + '=' + ('[withheld]' if GUARD.search(s) else s[:300]))
            else: kept(v, out)
    elif isinstance(o, list):
        for v in o: kept(v, out)

There is no pre_send_refusal: a refusal before sending is journaled as failed with authorization_does_not_cover, authorization_indeterminate, attestation_failed or identity_mismatch (PLANNER_FAILURES in planner-loop.ts). planner.plan_refused is written only when judgeRoundPlan refuses a plan (plan_invalid, plan_outside_captured_scope), and its reason kinds are schema, scope_path, encoding, not_ready and outside_captured_scope. not_json, schema_invalid, terminal_status_not_ready and scope_mismatch do not exist, and a plan the plan store refuses for credential material writes no such line.

  "data_flow": "The section will trace the end-to-end Phase 4 planner loop data flow as built:\n1. Scope capture (T1): A caller posts `scope.captured` to `POST /rooms/:roomId/events` with a `goal` string. `prepareFounderGoal` checks bounds and credential patterns, computes `founderGoalDigest(goal)`, attaches `{ kind: 'founder_goal', ref: event.eventId, digest }` to `evidence`, and stores `goal` in the room event payload (`packages/control-plane/src/founder-goal.ts` lines 47-72).\n2. Round opening and journal-before-dispatch (T2): `PlannerLoop.runRound` (in `packages/control-plane/src/planner-loop.ts`) reads the room state under the fenced leader store. In `SCOPED`, it resolves the seat via `resolvePlannerSeat()`, verifies the route via `authorizeRoute`, computes `plannerCommandIdFor(roomId, openingEventId, 1)`, and in one Postgres transaction appends T2 `task.dispatched.planner` (deriving `rolesAssigned`, `reviewerIsNotBuilder`, and fence-backed `gatewayOnline` on the server) and writes the command's `journaled` row (`JournalStore.journalCommand`).\n3. Gateway poll, attestation, and execution: `GatewayPlannerWorker` (`packages/gateway-daemon/src/planner-worker.ts`) polls `POST /gateway/planner/poll`. On `attest`, it queries `executor.attest(commandId)` and reports `{ role: 'architect', surface: 'antigravity', provider: 'google', model: 'gemini-4-argon', effort: 'high' }`; `PlannerLoop` verifies `role === 'architect'` and writes `identity_bound`. On `execute`, `PlannerRecordStore` writes a durable `accepted` record to `<gatewayDirectory>/planner/<sha256(commandId)>.json` before `AgyPlannerExecutor` verifies the root-owned `agy` binary SHA-256, prepares a read-only checkout at `baseSha` with `scopePaths`, spawns `sudo -n -u <plannerUser> -- <agyPath>` with `PINNED_AGY_ARGUMENTS`, waits for the first stream-json `init` line confirming `init.model === 'gemini-4-argon-high'` before writing the prompt to stdin, and extracts the single `plandoc/1` JSON object.\n4. Init verification, plan judgment, and atomic T3 commit: `PlannerLoop` requires `PlannerInit` (`initModel` and `conversationId`) for `antigravity` + `google`, checks observed vs intended route, and writes `dispatched` carrying `init_model` and `provider_conversation_id`. When `awaitPlan` returns the raw document, `judgeRoundPlan` runs `judgePlanDoc` (`PLANDOC_SCHEMA_V1`, `checkScopePaths`, `work_id === commandId`, `repository`, `base_sha`, `terminal_status === 'PLAN_READY_FOR_FOUNDER_APPROVAL'`). `submitRoundPlan` then commits T3 `plan.submitted` (with `plan_bundle` evidence carrying `planHash`), `PlanStore.recordPlan` (`public.build_room_plans`), and the journal `completed` row (`result_digest = planHash`) in one transaction.\n5. On T4, the next `PlannerLoop.runRound` in `PLANNING` loads the prior plan from `PlanStore`, verifies its `planHash` against T4's `revisingPlanHash`, and passes `earlierPlan` and `revisionFeedback` into the revision round.",
